### C2/D2 restart recovery — project image candidate worker

- Fixed a concrete durable-queue restart gap. On app startup, the project image queue now requeues only `preparing` jobs with no reserved ComfyUI prompt ID (provably not submitted) and marks any `submitting`/`running` job with a prompt ID `recovery_required`. An inconsistent `preparing` row that has a prompt ID is also quarantined. No ambiguous job is automatically resent.

## Continuation — 2026-10-02 (GPU-safe work only)

### C2 foundation — durable project image-candidate queue store (not yet wired to API/rendering)

- Added `services/production_image_jobs.py`, an additive SQLite store sharing the Production V2 ledger path. Candidate batches are project/run scoped, enforce 1–4 candidates, persist the exact compiled graph/settings/prompt/seed/version, and use an idempotency key so a retried request returns the same candidate batch instead of creating duplicate GPU work.
- The public job view omits worker-private graph JSON. Claiming is atomic and one candidate at a time. A ComfyUI prompt ID must be reserved before a job can become running. A restarted worker marks uncertain `submitting`/`running` rows `recovery_required`, preserving the prompt ID and explicitly forbidding automatic resubmission; it does not yet query/reconcile ComfyUI history.
- Added `tests/test_production_image_jobs.py`: batch persistence/idempotency, project scoping, changed-input conflict, sequential claim, stable prompt ID requirement, restart-safe recovery marking and invalid candidate rejection.
- Verification: image-job store + image workflow + Production V2 API focused tests — **59 passed**, 4 existing FastAPI lifecycle deprecation warnings.
- This is only the durable storage/queue foundation. It is **not yet connected** to a Production V2 endpoint, image renderer/ComfyUI worker, asset output registration, status polling, or UI; C2 remains partial. No GPU work or ComfyUI request occurred.

### C2 / C1 / D3 — project text-to-image candidate queue vertical slice

- Added the separate `production_image_jobs` SQLite table in the same durable V2 ledger file. API `POST .../runs/{run_id}/image-jobs` checks the project/run, exact workflow live-preflight/smoke availability and image role, compiles the graph, then durably queues four candidates only for Manual/Semi with user image-selection enabled. Full/Director-owned selection fails closed until automated visual review exists. Text-to-image (Z-Image Turbo and Qwen 2512) are dispatchable; Qwen Edit is explicitly rejected until safe project-image staging is implemented. Idempotent retries return the existing batch and do not create more GPU work.
- Added `ProductionImageWorker` and its idle-safe supervisor to app startup/shutdown. A candidate first checks `/queue`, then claims the shared cross-process submission lock, rechecks, reserves/persists its exact prompt UUID, submits exactly one ComfyUI graph, polls that prompt, copies the output into `output/<project>/production/image_jobs/<job_id>/`, registers it once in the project manifest, and reports status through the batch API. Busy/unreachable ComfyUI keeps the job in the website backend queue; uncertain submit/poll outcomes become `recovery_required` and are never blindly retried. The worker removes only Comfy output files whose exact prefix is recorded in its compiled `SaveImage` graph, after the canonical project asset is registered; unrelated/temp files are left untouched.
- Outputs initially register as `image_candidate`, not as accepted canon masters. Added an ID-preserving accept operation which appends the intended image role to the existing asset record without another media copy. Manual and Semi with the image-selection gate enabled can accept; Full or Director-owned Semi selection fails closed because an automated visual candidate reviewer is not yet integrated. The image job panel in the Production Assets stage exposes model, role, prompt, size/steps, candidate status and refreshes the canonical project asset shelf. No legacy page was changed.
- Tests cover durable batch/idempotency/project scoping, one-at-a-time claims, stable prompt ID, unknown-submit no-retry, queue-health deferral, successful mocked worker collection/provenance, prefix-only Comfy output cleanup, no-copy candidate promotion, API run/preflight policy, and browser queue/accept journey.

Frontend Vitest **1 passed**, build passed (existing Vite large-chunk/config warnings), ESLint **0 errors, 13 existing warnings**, targeted mocked Playwright Production workspace **1 passed**. Temporary Vite on 4180 was stopped. No live ComfyUI request, GPU render, service restart, Docker/Conda change, package install, or workflow modification occurred in this slice.

### C4 — run-scoped local dialogue conversion API slice

- Added a production-run endpoint that accepts only the existing local `voice_changer` and `rvc` operations for an uploaded original dialogue take. It checks the asset role/status, run ID, and unchanged character-to-S1/S2 binding before scheduling work through the existing local audio-effects worker; it does not add hosted conversion or download models.
- On successful worker completion, the FLAC export is registered as a distinct `dialogue_take` asset with the exact transcript, speaker/run/character provenance, source asset ID, operation, and job ID. The original recording is never replaced. Registration failure marks the effect job failed rather than silently leaving an untracked result.

New tests cover run-scoped input checks, rejected operation, scheduled local job, immutable source, and output lineage. No ComfyUI request or GPU job was made.

UI copy states that the selected RVC model is not the character's bound voice master and that models are never downloaded automatically.

### D2 / E1–E3 continuation — backend regression, browser sweep and operator handoff

- C4 recorded-dialogue foundation: added a run-scoped upload for the **original** exact spoken take. It requires an active canon character and that run's saved voice binding, then stores transcript, character/run IDs, and stable S1/S2 in structured asset metadata while retaining/auditioning the original audio. Duplicate bytes with conflicting transcript metadata fail closed. This does not yet perform TTS or local voice conversion; those remain explicit C4 gaps, and the UI states that conversion is separate.
- C4 verification: production asset/API subset — **48 passed**; Production V2 Playwright — **1 passed** including upload with exact transcript; full repository-owned backend suite — **289 passed**, 4 existing FastAPI lifecycle deprecation warnings; frontend build passed, Vitest **1 passed**, lint **0 errors, 13 existing warnings**. No GPU work was started.
- E1 launcher hardening: added a per-checkout `flock` so a second invocation exits before checking/starting dependencies; Python preflight for FastAPI/Uvicorn; bind-based backend/frontend port preflight with `ss` guidance; and Vite `--strictPort`. The lock descriptor is closed in child processes, so it is held by the launcher lifetime only. Ollama/ComfyUI health-check/start policy remains unchanged.
- E1 verification: `bash -n run_story_builder.sh` passed. Added `tests/test_run_story_builder_launcher.py`, which uses stubbed curl/backend/frontend commands (no real AI services or app workers started) to test missing Python modules, occupied port rejection before service checks, simultaneous launch refusal, and strict frontend port. **2 passed**. This verifies launcher control flow, not a live startup with the current GPU runtime; E1 remains partial until the normal local startup path is verified after ComfyUI/GPU recovery.
- D2 parent-retake dependency gate: acceptance now previews the exact queued/waiting child take IDs attached to superseded accepted takes. Confirmation must echo the previewed IDs; the ledger recomputes under one SQLite write transaction, refuses stale confirmation, and blocks acceptance if a dependent take is already submitted/running/recovering. On confirmed acceptance, only queued descendants of old parents become stale, accepted descendants remain intact, and children waiting on the newly accepted take are released to `queued`. Generic state transitions can no longer bypass this acceptance path. Added API/UI coverage and durable event records for each state change.

### B3 / D1 — persisted Refine proposal API (first implementation slice)

- Added `services/production_refine_store.py` for atomically stored run-scoped Refine proposals under the validated production revision root.
- Added `POST /api/projects/{project_id}/production/v2/runs/{run_id}/refine`: it requires an accepted shot-plan revision, resolves the selected shot from that saved revision, and binds the LLM proposal to the exact prompt, assets, reference map and revision. The proposal is persisted and is non-mutating.
- Added `POST /api/projects/{project_id}/production/v2/runs/{run_id}/refine/{proposal_id}/accept`: stale revision/prompt checks and H3 lint are required. Acceptance writes a new shot-plan revision with parent linkage; the source revision remains unchanged. Fully automated runs cannot bypass Director review through this route.
- Added `tests/test_production_refine_api.py` for persistence/non-mutation, accepted new revision, and stale prompt rejection.

## Card B4 — production-type Director profile foundation

- Status: partial implementation; additive versioned profiles are integrated into new V2 run snapshots and passed into story expansion, Full-mode Director review, and downstream text-stage context. Text-only source extraction/upload/listing, cited style proposals, draft revisions, immutable published versions, forks, archive state, catalog listing, and new-run variant snapshots are implemented and tested. Style Library UI/Playwright and additional evaluation fixtures remain outstanding.
- Added `prompts/director_profiles/production_types_v1.json` with materially distinct behavior/review priorities for all six existing production types: story/film, social/short-form, corporate pitch, informative/educational, news/reporting, and advertisement. Existing `prompts/styles/*.json` files and legacy style routes were not edited.
- Added `services/production_director_profiles.py` to validate the registry, produce a canonical content hash, resolve an allowed production type, and fail closed for missing/invalid profile data.
- Added additive `GET /api/production/v2/director-profiles` and nested `GET /api/production/v2/styles`. New run creation validates `production_type` and snapshots the exact profile content/version/hash. Story expansion and Director review receive that frozen profile; generated story and downstream text revisions record its version/hash. Downstream generation context includes the same profile. The nested catalog joins each of the six unchanged legacy style packs to its Director profile and currently returns an empty `variants` list until variant storage is implemented.
- Added `tests/test_production_director_profiles.py` and `tests/test_production_style_catalog.py` plus API assertions for all six distinct profiles, hash/version, invalid type, registry validation, profile delivery into story generation, and legacy route compatibility.
- Added `services/narrative_style_sources.py`: accept only PDF/Markdown/TXT, bound bytes/extracted characters and extraction time, reject empty/invalid inputs, extract PDF with `pdftotext` via stdin/stdout (no user filename passed as a path/command), and return page/line evidence locators. Hash-addressed source storage keeps one raw byte copy, verifies integrity on deduplication/list, and stores compact metadata under `storage/production/style_library/sources/`.
- Added `services/narrative_style_library.py`: validates stage coverage, override fields, source IDs, exact cited excerpts/locators, confidence and bounded payload size; supports draft creation/edit with expected-revision checks, immutable published JSON versions with content hashes, fork-to-next-version, archive index state, and active-version resolution. Published content hash tampering is detected. A version fork does not modify its parent version.
- Added `/api/production/v2/styles/analyze` to create a provider-generated **unpublished, non-persisted** proposal from up to five selected sources; citations must match the stored extracted blocks exactly. Added additive draft create/update, publish, fork, archive, variant-list routes. `/api/production/v2/styles` now nests currently published variants; new production runs may select a published non-archived variant only when its base matches the production type, and snapshot the full immutable version/hash. Story expansion, Full review, and each downstream text stage receive only the relevant resolved stage rules alongside the type-specific Director profile. Legacy `/api/automation/styles` remains unchanged.
- Added `tests/test_narrative_style_library.py` plus API integration coverage for source-to-proposal verification, draft optimistic concurrency, immutable v1/v2 publishing/forking, archive behavior, run snapshotting, and compatibility.

### D2 queue ownership hardening — backend-owned admission (2026-10-02)

- User-provided `nvidia-smi` snapshot showed ComfyUI Python at 40,977 MiB and another Python compute process at 4,354 MiB, but only 2% instantaneous GPU utilization. A read-only ComfyUI `/queue` check returned empty at the time checked. This means substantial VRAM is allocated/resident but the sample does not prove active generation or that a queued prompt caused the allocation.
- Audit confirmed the Production V2 ledger already atomically claims at most one active V2 take globally; further takes remain `queued` in the website database. Gap: the worker posted to ComfyUI without first checking whether another ComfyUI client/job had occupied its own queue.
- Hardened the V2 worker to fail closed: it queries `/queue` after claiming a backend job and again immediately before submit. If ComfyUI reports running/pending work, or queue state is unavailable/malformed, it does not POST `/prompt`; a safe pre-prompt-ID claim is returned to the website queue. If work became busy during staging, only the attempt-owned staged inputs are cleaned before requeue. Added tests for busy-at-admission, busy-during-staging, no prompt submission, and safe queued state.
- Verification: `PYTHONPATH=/home/riki/web_dev PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE=1 python3 -m pytest -q -p no:cacheprovider tests/test_production_job_worker.py tests/test_production_ledger.py` — 21 passed.
- Follow-up found that legacy generation features also submit through `services.media_jobs.submit_and_wait`. Added a shared cross-thread/process `comfy_submission_guard` there and used the same guard for Production V2. The helper checks ComfyUI `/queue` and waits backend-side for idle before posting, then holds admission through render completion. Production V2 rechecks while holding the guard; if an external/manual Comfy job arrived during preparation, it cleans only its own staging and returns the take to the durable website ledger. New tests cover busy queue deferral, fail-closed malformed/offline queue state, safe requeue and website submission ordering.
- Updated scope truth: concurrent Story Builder submissions now serialize through the shared guard and do not pile up in ComfyUI. Legacy feature records do not yet share a single durable global queue/position/cancel API, but their submitters are serialized. A manual ComfyUI user is external and can still race after an idle snapshot; the next website admission detects visible work and waits/defer rather than clearing it.
- Final verification for this queue slice: `tests/test_media_jobs.py tests/test_production_v2_api.py tests/test_production_job_worker.py tests/test_production_take_runtime.py tests/test_production_ledger.py tests/test_production_shot_validation.py tests/test_production_assets.py` — 79 passed, 4 existing FastAPI lifecycle deprecation warnings. ### Production worker guard and image prompt recovery (2026-10-04)

- Added shared GPU operating-point sampling (temperature and graphics clock) and applied a one-second watchdog in the Production V2 video-take and project-image workers. Defaults use the established operator policy: interrupt threshold 83°C (margin below85°C maximum), graphics-clock ceiling2100MHz, and durable per-sample telemetry. When exceeded, a worker calls ComfyUI `/interrupt` only after verifying that its exact saved prompt is the sole running item and no prompt is pending; otherwise it preserves `recovery_required` with an explicit refusal. This targeted ownership check reduces risk but ComfyUI interrupt is a global active-prompt operation and cannot eliminate a race with manual/external ComfyUI clients.
- Added dependency injection for both full GPU admission inspection and operating-point reads to make worker state machines isolatable in later tests. Tests were not run for this slice per the user's instruction to focus on coding.
- Added image-worker exact-prompt recovery. Startup-held jobs now check their saved `/history/{prompt_id}` plus the queue; completed outputs are collected and registered through the existing hash-deduplicating asset registry, failures are marked failed, active or absent prompts remain held, and no prompt is resubmitted. The image supervisor reconciles saved prompts before claiming fresh jobs. GPU-admission deferrals remain backend-queued with a bounded wait.
- Required source backup: `/tmp/story-builder-worker-recovery-before-20261004.tar.gz` (services/production_image_jobs.py, production_image_worker.py, production_job_worker.py, api/main.py, implementation log). Restore selected files with `tar -xzf ... -C /home/riki/web_dev/story_builder`.
- Static Python compilation succeeded for the changed worker/runtime modules and API entrypoint. No tests, ComfyUI startup, GPU render, model load, or service changes were made. `git status` remains unavailable because the checkout's `.git` directory is not a valid repository.

### C2 project-image staging and workflow readiness — 2026-10-04

- Added the website Qwen Image Edit 2511 source flow. The request accepts one to three project asset IDs; the API validates project ownership and image type, rejects unaccepted generated candidates and duplicate IDs, resolves paths through the private asset registry, and copies source bytes with the existing H3 hash-verified owner-manifest staging helper. The compiled workflow stores only safe staged basenames; durable job settings retain source asset IDs and an internal staging owner, while public responses redact that owner.
- Image source staging is deterministic and idempotent for the same source ID/hash. Durable candidate provenance retains each source asset ID and SHA-256, while staged bytes remain under the ComfyUI input owner manifest. Inputs remain until every candidate reaches completed/failed/cancelled; recovery-required or uncertain submissions retain staging. If a completed batch is retried with the same idempotency key, the staging copy is cleaned immediately after the durable store returns the original settled batch. Validation is compiled against placeholder names before source bytes are staged.
- The image model selector now consumes the existing exact workflow catalog and disables any adapter that is not available. Qwen Edit's settings default to 1024×1024/4 steps and the UI requires 1–3 selectable project images. Director-owned image selection remains blocked because visual review/retake control is still not implemented. No 2511 GPU render was started; catalog eligibility remains bound to its exact live smoke record.

# C4 conversion durability repair — 2026-10-04

The production dialogue conversion endpoint now enqueues into the authoritative `AudioSidecarStore` with project/run-scoped idempotency, source asset identity and a reserved ComfyUI prompt ID persisted before the submission boundary. The startup consumer owns conversion execution and exact-prompt recovery. A persisted `preparing` job may rebuild before the POST boundary with the same reserved ID; a persisted `submitting` job only observes that exact prompt after restart and never resubmits an ambiguous attempt. It retains its private ComfyUI input while the remote state is uncertain and removes only the job's exact staged input once the prompt is known to have failed or completed. Completed output registers as a separate `dialogue_take` asset with original source ID, stable character/speaker IDs, exact transcript and conversion operation. The original recording stays intact.

The conversion worker now uses the shared cross-process ComfyUI admission lock instead of its process-local audio-only lock. This preserves one Story Builder GPU submission at a time and reuses the common thermal watchdog/telemetry checks. The workspace polls the durable run-scoped job, holds the idempotency key through an unknown response, and disables duplicate conversion while the job is unsettled. The browser fixture now follows the production durable status route.

### 2026-10-04 — D2/D4 atomic Manual child release and stale-draft hold

- Defect found during plan audit: parent acceptance previously queued every waiting child without checking saved control mode or comparing the latest composer draft with the child's frozen request. A user edit after queue could also leave stale work claimable.
- `services/production_ledger.py` now gates parent release in one SQLite write transaction. Only unchanged Manual children with matching shot-plan revision and frozen request enter `queued`; changed/missing/stale drafts and all Semi/Full children enter `waiting_for_user`. Saved composer revisions and unclaimed-child holds are now one atomic ledger operation, serialized with the worker claim transaction. Unclaimed changed children cannot race into submission. Held children are included in parent-retake invalidation. No prompt/input snapshot is rewritten.
- `api/main.py` uses this atomic save path and returns held take IDs. `ProductionWorkspace.tsx` shows an actionable notice after such a save. Playwright covers same-scene dependency and edited-after-queue review notice.
- Verification: focused ledger/API suites **68 passed**; full backend suite **411 passed, 4 warnings**; frontend typecheck, touched ESLint, Vitest **1 passed**, build passed, full Playwright **20 passed**. Playwright traces/results: `/tmp/story-builder-playwright-final-20261004`.

### 2026-10-04 — D2 exact-prompt cancellation repair

- Fixed a state-machine/API mismatch: API cancellation accepted `recovery_required`, but the ledger rejected the transition to `cancel_requested`. The transition is now legal.
- Running cancellation calls `interrupt_if_owned_prompt`, which re-reads `/queue` and requests ComfyUI's global interrupt only when this exact prompt is the sole running item and no prompt is pending. If ownership cannot be proved, the take remains `cancel_requested` for exact-prompt reconciliation. An interrupt acknowledgment is not treated as cancellation completion. Queued prompt deletion is unchanged.
- Regression coverage exercises a sole-owner running prompt and a refused guard from `recovery_required`; existing GPU runtime/watchdog tests cover foreign or ambiguous queue refusal.

### 2026-10-04 — direct Video Repertoire analyzer cancellation ownership

- Added the durable analysis job ID as a Docker label to PEAV and InternVideo3 analyzer containers. Cancellation stops only containers returned for that exact label, then confirms the label has no running containers. It performs a second scan after worker exit to close the race where the worker could start another Docker stage between the initial scan and process termination.
- Persisted worker PIDs are signaled only after verifying process-group leadership and `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` identity for the exact worker kind/job. The API leaves cancellation pending if Docker/process ownership or exit cannot be verified; it does not claim cancellation based on a signal request. Worker progress and failure writes cannot replace `cancel_requested`.

### 2026-10-04 — C2 Director review leases and truthful recovery polling

- The image-job SQLite store now serializes Full-mode candidate review claims across backend instances. A reviewer owns a 40-minute lease; stale work can be reclaimed up to three attempts and then receives an explicit durable blocked decision. # Durable revision/task crash reconciliation — 2026-10-04

Continued B1 recovery work. Queued story and text worker payloads now stamp canonical revisions with their durable stage-task UUID and request hash. When lease recovery is observed, the API searches only the matching run/stage, requires one exact task/hash/project/run match, reloads the canonical file, and settles SQLite as completed under the task execution fence. A missing or ambiguous match remains `recovery_required` for explicit operator inspection. This prevents a restart between revision persistence and task settlement from needlessly repeating inference.

Tests simulate the persisted-revision/unsettled-task window for both story and scenes and confirm status polling restores the existing revision without another provider call. Focused backend suites: **65 passed**; full maintained backend suite: **439 passed**, four existing FastAPI lifecycle warnings. No GPU workload was submitted; this change is text task persistence only. Recovery archive: `/tmp/story_builder_controller_reconcile_preedit_20261004.tar.gz`, SHA-256 `f9a975ec15068f755ab0aedc41b507b1753b05e4075ce9bc4cde68fc77d6b31b`; inspect with `tar -tzf` and selectively restore from `/home/riki/web_dev/story_builder` if necessary.

# Zero-reference H3 T2V route, monitored worker acceptance, and settlement repair — 2026-10-04

Added a separately capability-gated H3 text-to-video route for direct-H3 shots with no image/video/audio references. The R2V route remains unchanged for referenced shots. The compiler validates the installed graph and 5–10 second T2V limit; the capability must see exact model/node readiness, the feature flag, and an intact hash-verified H.264/AAC smoke artifact. Production V2 preview, queue and worker use the T2V workflow ID. The shot composer now identifies T2V versus R2V, offers 0.98/0.4 presets, caps the empty-reference UI at 10 seconds, and wraps the validation hash on mobile.

### Provider process-death handling and accepted-story ancestry — 2026-10-04

- `services/reasoning_provider.py` now starts Linux provider commands through `services/provider_exec_guard.py`, a small exec wrapper that arms `PR_SET_PDEATHSIG` and checks the parent PID to close the setup race. Provider commands run in an owned process group; timeout handling sends TERM, waits five seconds, then escalates to KILL. This prevents a local Codex CLI from continuing after an abruptly killed backend. It cannot prove or cancel remote inference after the provider server has accepted a request, and ambiguous tasks remain held for explicit recovery.
- The integration test ran a fake CLI in a separate worker, killed the worker with SIGKILL, and verified the provider process left the running state. Provider tests **6 passed**; worker and Production V2 API suites **69 passed**.
- A live provider test used an isolated temporary backend, temporary project/ledger/output, and the real Codex CLI 0.160.0; the user app was untouched. Codex exited before inference with an error reading `/home/riki/.codex/rules/default.rules:488` (Starlark parse error at an embedded NUL). The task was observed as `failed`, attempt 1, with a retryable config error. No real inference or backend-kill-during-inference occurred, so B1 live restart remains open. Byte inspection showed line 488 contains 181 NUL bytes and a newline, with no other content. The minimal repair proposal is deleting only that NUL-only line; the user file remains unchanged pending authorization.
- `accept_production_v2_text_revision` now confirms the saved revision identity, accepted story ancestor, matching hash, and newest accepted story revision before accepting downstream text. Regression captures the same original source hash across two different accepted expansions and proves downstream text from the older ancestor gets HTTP 409. Focused cases **3 passed**; full API test module **60 passed**; full backend suite excluding only the launcher test module **457 passed** with four existing FastAPI lifecycle warnings. The two excluded launcher tests cannot run while the user app owns the duplicate-start lock; a full attempt had 456 passed and those two expected refusals.

### Live cancellation settlement correction — 2026-10-04

- First live attempt exposed two bugs: successful ComfyUI `/interrupt` returns an empty body, which the JSON request helper treated as a decode failure; after that, `execution_interrupted` history was recorded as `failed`. A follow-up fixed acknowledgment parsing and persisted `cancel_requested` before sending the interrupt, then exposed a concurrent supervisor-reconciliation race that still labeled the take `comfy_render_failed_after_restart`. Both failures were preserved as evidence; no automatic retry was issued.
- Corrected the shared worker/supervisor interpretation of ComfyUI `execution_interrupted`: only when the durable take is `cancel_requested` does remote error history settle as `cancelled`; all other remote execution errors remain `failed`. Terminal reconciliation now sets completion time and releases the worker lease. ComfyUI's empty successful response body is accepted as an empty acknowledgment; callers still validate required response fields.
- Added regressions for empty successful HTTP responses, cancellation intent being recorded before the interrupt guard runs, normal worker cancel settlement, and concurrent supervisor reconciliation. Focused worker/ledger/API tests: **5 passed**. Full maintained backend suite: **470 passed**, four existing FastAPI lifecycle warnings. An initial new regression assertion assumed null error keys are returned; the test was corrected to match the API's omission of absent optional fields.

ComfyUI history recorded `execution_interrupted`; durable status settled `cancelled`, attempt 1, no error, no lease, and terminal timestamp. No media output was registered. The independent 2-second telemetry sampler peaked at **52°C**, **81% utilization**, and **2086 MHz**; intervention cutoff remained 83°C and user maximum 85°C. Afterwards the queue was empty, ComfyUI was the only compute owner, and memory was above reserve (**84,807,114,240 bytes free**); GPU was 49°C / 8% / 2086 MHz. ### D4 browser-order test stabilization — 2026-10-04

- Focused reproduction showed that `page.click()` returned before the async queue handler completed. The request-order array was read synchronously, so the test could fail with `indexOf("queue") === -1` even though the page subsequently reported a successful queued take. Replaced the immediate check with `expect.poll` waiting for the queue request, then kept the strict assertion that the draft-save request immediately precedes it.
- Focused Production Workspace scenario passed **3/3 consecutive runs**. Full mocked Playwright: **21 passed, 1 skipped** (opt-in live saved-take test lacked explicit environment variables). Frontend typecheck passed; Vitest **11 passed**; ESLint **0 errors / 13 existing warnings**; production build passed with existing Vite config-loader and chunk-size warnings. ### Prompt-preparation lost-response idempotency — 2026-10-05

- Review found the browser generated a new prompt-task idempotency key for every attempt and persisted the task ID only after a successful response. A server-side commit followed by a network timeout could therefore create duplicate provider tasks when the user retried. The browser now writes the key before POST and stores it with a fingerprint of the exact request; an unchanged retry reuses that key, while changed input gets a fresh key. The returned task ID is still stored for normal reload polling.

### Accepted-prefix Full/delegated-Semi progression and same-scene continuity — 2026-10-05

- Replaced the controller's “stop after any take” behavior with a strict accepted-prefix scheduler. It schedules one next shot only after the prior take for every earlier planned shot is accepted; it stops on an unknown shot, duplicate/retake row, unsettled/failed status, or a take from another shot-plan revision. Same-scene next shots bind the immediate predecessor take; first shots and scene boundaries have no parent.
- Added a `previous_cut_tail` role to registered H3 video outputs as eligibility metadata only. The general approved catalog still excludes unaccepted outputs. The controller exposes a tail only after verifying the accepted take, immediate prior shot in the same scene, project/run/shot/take registry metadata, and SHA-256 against its canonical output row. Prompt-preparation snapshots and queue requests bind the predecessor; take queueing rechecks the exact asset. Cross-scene inheritance and altered preparation/parent relationships fail closed.
- Review found a related ledger idempotency defect: the same key and input snapshot with a changed `parent_take_id` previously returned the original row without conflict. The ledger now includes parent lineage in replay equality, with regression coverage for same-parent replay and changed-parent rejection.
- Updated quality gates and Sol/Luna restart instructions to require accepted-prefix progression, current revision binding, same-scene provenance and boundary reset, explicit negative cases, and a closed Director render gate until live worker/review/browser acceptance.

Targeted Playwright `production-auto-take-recovery.spec.ts` **1 passed**; the prior full mocked suite remains **22 passed, 1 skipped**. Browser server was stopped. Initial sandbox runs hit socket denials and read-only Vite temp writes; reruns with local permissions passed. No configured-provider inference, ComfyUI prompt, or GPU workload was run.
